```text
The evaluate.py is 37775 bytes. The C009 constraint mentions "westd 37591 bytes 版本" as the reference hash. This rerun uses a 37775-byte version, which is different from the reference version! This is a potential concern — but the script did run successfully, and the d000/ARC values all match the paper exactly, so maybe this version difference is benign or the reference hash was updated.

Actually wait — C009 says "md5sum evaluate.py 并确认与 reference hash（westd 37591 bytes 版本）一致". The rerun version is 37775 bytes, which is different. 

The md5 hash is `17673bf373f00f83ccc41fc1d355a4cd`, but I shouldn't get too caught up in this — what matters is whether all 10 evaluations used the same script, which they clearly did since they ran sequentially. The real puzzle is why d025 and d100 MMLU diverge from the paper while d000, d005, and d050 align closely, suggesting the paper may have used that older 37591-byte version.

ARC matching perfectly across all dosages while MMLU shows selective discrepancies points to something specific about those checkpoints or how MMLU is evaluated. The script version difference could explain it if the older evaluate.py handled MMLU's mc_suffix differently without affecting ARC's distinct prompt format. I have enough to write this up now.

The key findings are that d000 MMLU is actually 53.79%, not the 58.60% from the paper; ARC stays consistent across all five dosages; MMLU shows minor variance in d000 and d050 but significant drops in d025 and d100; and the evaluate.py versions differ by 184 bytes, likely from that mc_suffix fix. Looking at the gains, my rerun shows a 3.21 percentage point improvement from d000 to d100, while the paper reported 4.81 points—both still demonstrate clear benefit, just with different magnitudes.

The dose-response curves reveal an interesting shift: the paper showed a dip at d050 before recovering at d100, but my rerun plateaus after d005 and then dips slightly at d100 instead. This suggests a more gradual saturation pattern rather than the paper's recovery trajectory, with that final dip at d100 being particularly noteworthy.

MMLU d025/d100 存在显著偏差\n- d025: rerun 56.17% vs paper 57.73%，低 1.56pp\n- d100: rerun 57.00% vs paper 58.60%，低 1.60pp\n\n可能原因：\n- evaluate.py 版本差异（37775 vs 37591 bytes）可能影响 MMLU 的 mc_suffix 处理（参考 C009/C010 的 mc_suffix 位置差异），但不影响 ARC（不同 prompt format）\n- d000/d005/d050 匹配良好而 d025/d100 偏低，说明不是全局系统性偏移，可能与特定 dosage 的 adapter 在不同 mc_suffix 位置下的敏感度有关\n\n### 4. Rerun MMLU 剂量-响应曲线\n```\nPaper:  d000(53.79) → d005(55.89) → d025(57.73) → d050(57.44) → d100(58.60)\nRerun:  d000(53.79) → d005(56.14) → d025(56.17) → d050(57.25) → d100(57.00)\n```\n- Paper 曲线：持续上升（d050 有小 dip），d000→d100 增幅 4.81pp\n- Rerun 曲线：d005 后 plateau，d050 后轻微回落，d000→d100 增幅 3.21pp\n- 两个曲线都显示 contamination 带来明确增益\n\n## 结论\n\n### d000 基线\n**验证通过**。d000 MMLU = 53.79% 是正确值，确认原始数据无误。\n\n### MMLU d025/d100 偏差\n需要进一步排查 evaluate.py 版本差异的具体影响。建议：用论文原始 evaluate.py（37591 bytes 版本）对 d025 和 d100 重新 eval，确认偏差是否来自脚本版本。\n\n### Pattern 分类\nOLMo-2-7B 在 MMLU 和 ARC 上均为 **Benefit** pattern：\n- MMLU: d000→d100 增幅 3.21pp (rerun) / 4.81pp (paper)，两者都远超 Immune 阈值\n- ARC: d000→d100 增幅 3.92pp，精确匹配\n- **无需修改 pattern 分类**\n"

## 结果摘要

**d000 MMLU 基线验证通过**：53.79% 精确匹配，确认不是 58.60%。

**ARC**：5 个 dosage 全部与论文值精确匹配（0.00pp 偏差）。

**MMLU 偏差**：

| Dosage | Paper | Rerun | Δ |
|--------|-------|-------|---|
| d000 | 53.79 | 53.79 | 0.00 ✅ |
| d005 | 55.89 | 56.14 | +0.25 ✅ |
| d025 | 57.73 | 56.17 | **-1.56** ⚠️ |
| d050 | 57.44 | 57.25 | -0.19 ✅ |
| d100 | 58.60 | 57.00 | **-1.60** ⚠️ |

d025 和 d100 的 MMLU 偏低 ~1.6pp，可能原因是 evaluate.py 版本差异（rerun 37775 bytes vs reference 37591 bytes），mc_suffix 处理差异只影响 MMLU 不影响 ARC。

**Pattern 分类**：OLMo-2 仍为 **Benefit**，无需修改。rerun MMLU 增幅 3.21pp（paper 4.81pp），ARC 增幅 3.92pp 完全一致。

**建议下一步**：用 37591-byte reference evaluate.py 对 d025/d100 做验证 eval，确认偏差来源。
